---
name: using-superpowers
description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring Skill tool invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions
---

<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, skip this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. This is not optional. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) — highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** — override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** — lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to you—follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" → brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" → debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

Also: I considered keeping `c3-2`'s inner-element selectors for "back-compat" but that would have reintroduced coexistence (which was exactly what cf-19 R2 P3 was working around). The user's directive "出现问题会直接让你推翻重写" maps directly to "no back-compat for the wrong-shape DOM"; updating tests to track new authority is the correct way.

docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:139:**What I did**: Composed all 8 existing drag/drop primitives (`edge-rects`, `tiebreak`, `outline-overlay`, `drag-ghost`, `drop-pulse`, `esc-cancel`, `layout-reducer`, `apply-drop-mode`) plus cf-20c-1 algebra into the actual interactive drag wire. Q7 spike FIRST: wrote a throwaway Playwright spec injecting a `<button draggable=true>` into the cf-19 `.skb-block-nodeview__gutter` with synthetic `DragEvent` dispatches; verified HTML5 native DnD survives ProseMirror inside `contenteditable=false` (PM didn't intercept; dragstart + dragover + drop all fired with our `dataTransfer` payload intact). Decision: ship HTML5 native DnD, save ~150 LOC of pointer-events fallback. Built the integration: `DragHandleButton` (presentational), `DragDropContext` + `DragDropProvider` (React context bridge), `useDragDropPipeline()` (lifecycle owner — snapshot + edge-rects + tiebreak + applyDropMode + Tiptap setNodeMarkup). `BlockNodeView.tsx` renders the per-block button inside the cf-19 gutter shell with `blockId = String(getPos())` per cf-20c-2 D2 path A. `EditorShellMount.tsx` mounts the pipeline + provider + `<OutlineOverlay>` + `<DragGhost>` overlay siblings; removes the obsolete cf-19 standalone `<DragHandle />`. Added 6 vitest tests for the button + 2 Playwright integration tests (desktop drag lifecycle + mobile-hidden lock per cf-20b R1 + ADR-0017 D9). All 185 editor-shell + 78 apps/site + 55 Playwright (was 53; +2 cf-20c-2) pass; 41/41 pnpm check successful; 0 lint errors.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:143:**What I'd do differently**: I almost wrote `useDragDropPipeline()` to dispatch Tiptap commands during `dragover` (mid-drag preview mutation), which would have violated ADR-0016 D11's "grid attrs are passive data on NodeView; mutations flow through reducer at drop only" contract. Caught it during the JSDoc-writing pass for the pipeline file when I re-read the ADR. Added an explicit comment in the pipeline JSDoc: "snapshot at drag-start, mutate at drop, NEVER mid-drag". For cf-20d (resize) I'll re-read ADR-0016 D11 + D12 BEFORE writing the resize-mid-drag code path; the same temptation will appear there (live-preview resize as cursor moves vs commit-on-release).
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:145:**Operational rule landed**: **For interactive layouts that snapshot state at drag/resize start, READ ADR-0016 D11 + D12 BEFORE writing the lifecycle code.** The temptation is to do live-preview mutation (update node.attrs as cursor moves so the user sees the layout change in real time). ADR-0016 explicitly forbids this — mutations flow through the layoutReducer ONLY at drop-success (cf-20c-2 dispatches `setNodeMarkup` exactly once per drop, never mid-drag). The visual preview comes from `<OutlineOverlay>` (a separate React subtree) drawing dashed accents over the affected edges; the actual block stays at its baseline position throughout the drag, then snaps to the new position via Tiptap state update at drop. This pattern applies to cf-20d resize handles AND cf-20e kebab-menu confirm dialogs — keep the live preview cosmetic; commit at terminal action.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:151:## ux-ui-lead reflection — cf-20c-2 R1 hotfix (4 findings: source-lift + DropPulse mount + velocity unit + terminal-drop spec) (2026-05-09)
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:154:F1 (HIGH, source-lift): pipeline `onDragStart` filters source out of edge-rects (`blocks.filter(b => b.id !== blockId)` BEFORE `computeEdgeRects`); DragDropContext exposes `sourceBlockId` so `BlockNodeView.tsx` applies `.skb-block-nodeview--dragging-self` modifier class; CSS rule per v2 contract (opacity 0.28 + grayscale 0.4 + dashed outline + pointer-events: none).
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:155:F2 (HIGH, DropPulse): pipeline exposes `clearLastDropped()`; `EditorShellMount.tsx` mounts `<DropPulseAtRect rect={blockRects.get(lastDroppedBlockId)} onAnimationEnd={clearLastDropped} />` whenever `lastDroppedBlockId !== null` — the rect comes from the snapshotted blockRects so the pulse appears at the source's pre-drag position.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:156:F3 (MEDIUM, velocity unit): pipeline multiplies raw `delta px / delta ms` by `VELOCITY_WINDOW_MS = 16` so `tiebreak()` reads `vx`/`vy` in `px/frame at 60fps` per ADR-0017 D3 contract.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:157:F4 (MEDIUM, terminal-drop spec): NEW Playwright test "cf-20c-2 R1 F4 — terminal drop" exercises full lifecycle (dragstart → dragover → rAF → drop) and asserts source's gridColumn mutates from `1 / span 12` to `7 / span 6` (split-right algebra) AND DropPulse anchor mounts.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:158:All 186 vitest + 78 site vitest + 57 Playwright pass; 41/41 pnpm check successful; 0 lint errors. cf-20c-2 R0 missed a real ADR-0017 D6 spec requirement; R1 closed it + the DropPulse mount-vs-doc-claim gap + the unit mismatch + the integration test coverage gap.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:160:**What surprised me / what I missed**: The most embarrassing one was F2 (DropPulse claim-vs-reality). cf-20c-2 R0's CONTRACT.md said "lastDroppedBlockId drives <DropPulse> mounts at the consumer layer". The consumer layer (EditorShellMount.tsx) had no `<DropPulse>` mount whatsoever. I'd written that doc claim while planning the architecture, then never actually wired the mount. The reviewer correctly called it out as either ship-the-wire OR fix-the-doc-lie. **Lesson**: when authoring CONTRACT.md / JSDoc claims about cross-component wiring, the doc claim should be the LAST thing written, after the wire is verified end-to-end. Pre-emptive doc-as-spec is fine for design docs (ADR-0017 D11 specifies the contract); for implementation docs (CONTRACT.md "this consumer renders that"), claim only what tests verify.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:162:The next surprise was F4's `requestAnimationFrame` timing requirement. My first version of the terminal-drop test dispatched dragover + drop synchronously inside the same `page.evaluate()`. It failed because the pipeline's drop handler closure captures `activeMatch` from React state — synchronous dispatch reads `activeMatch === null` (the closure was bound when `useEffect` ran during `setActive(true)` at dragstart, BEFORE dragover's `setActiveMatch(winner)` had committed). The drop dispatched `drag-end-mode-none` (rollback), source's gridColumn stayed at `1 / span 12`, the assertion failed. Cost ~30 min to debug via probe spec. **The same bug class would catch ANY future Playwright test of React-state-driven event chains — cf-20d resize handles, cf-20e kebab actions, cf-22 keyboard sequences.** Operational rule landed in cf-20c-2 PR.md D11.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:164:**What I'd do differently**: For F1 source-lift, ADR-0017 D6 was explicit and I read it as "source visually lifts" but mentally translated that to "the DragGhost becomes the source's visual stand-in". The ADR also requires the source NodeView itself to enter a lifted state (the ghost is a separate floating element AT cursor; the source NodeView at original position needs the dimmed/grayscale visual). I conflated "source disappears" with "ghost replaces source visually". The reviewer's grep for `.dragging-self` CSS class was the structural test that caught my conflation. **For ADR-derived spec features, list each D-decision's required artifacts (CSS class, DOM mutation, edge-rect filter, etc.) BEFORE coding so I don't ship 3-of-4 silently.** Cost of NOT having that list: cf-20c-2 R0 shipped passing tests but missing a real D6 requirement; R1 added 3 new code locations + 1 new spec to backfill.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:166:**Operational rule landed**: **For any PR that claims to implement an ADR D-decision, write down ALL required artifacts BEFORE coding, then check each one off as the implementation lands.** ADR-0017 D6 source-lift required: (a) source excluded from edge-rects [missed in R0; landed R1]; (b) source NodeView visually dimmed [missed in R0; landed R1]; (c) source NodeView non-droppable target [defense-in-depth via pointer-events: none in R1 CSS; not strictly required by D6 but cheap insurance]; (d) regression-lock spec [missed in R0; landed R1]. If I'd had this checklist at cf-20c-2 PLAN, all 4 artifacts would have shipped in R0. Adding to ux-ui-lead's pre-EXECUTE protocol: every ADR-derived feature gets an artifact-checklist drafted in PR.md "Decisions" section.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:168:**Operational rule landed (CONTRACT.md / JSDoc honesty)**: **CONTRACT.md and JSDoc claims about cross-component wiring MUST be written AFTER the wire is verified end-to-end, not before.** Pre-emptive doc-as-spec produces R-round drift when reality lags spec. ADR-style design docs are fine to spec ahead; implementation docs document what tests verify. cf-20c-2 R0 violated this twice: (a) DropPulse mount claim vs missing mount; (b) ADR-0017 D6 source-lift claim vs missing implementation. R1 fixed both via actual implementation; the doc rewrite would have caught the gaps if I'd been writing CONTRACT.md last instead of in parallel.
docs/orchestrator-reflections/2026-05-09-cf-15a-19-retrospective.md:170:**Bonus surprise (Playwright spec timing class)**: the React-state-driven event-chain timing bug from F4 generalizes. Any spec that fires event A and expects event B to read state set by A's React handler will fail without `await page.waitForTimeout(20)` or `requestAnimationFrame` between the two. cf-20c-2 R1 spec uses rAF inside `page.evaluate`. cf-20d resize will need the same pattern (mousedown sets `resizing=true`, mousemove reads it). Documented in cf-20c-2 PR.md D11.

docs/runbooks/codex-tool-invocations.md:87:- **R7 self-recursion mitigation**: never `tee` codex stdout into a path inside the workspace tree (`docs/...`, `.codex-runs/...`, `packages/...`, etc.); codex's workspace-write sandbox can see and re-patch its own audit log into a 50 MB+ self-referential growth loop within 10–15 min. packages/block-foundation/CONTRACT.md:43:- **W5-1: Grid context dimensions ↔ colSpan/rowSpan 联动** ([ADR-0016](../../docs/decisions/ADR-0016-grid-data-model.md)): 任意 block 在 grid context 渲染时, 其 visible bounding box dims 由 `{col, row?, colSpan, rowSpan}` (per ADR-0016 D2 `BlockGridPosition` schema) 唯一决定: `width(px) = colSpan * (1fr) + (colSpan - 1) * gap` (1fr 由 12-col / 6-col / 1-col 响应式动态计算 per ADR-0016 D5); `height(px) = rowSpan * row-h + (rowSpan - 1) * gap = rowSpan * 62 - 14` (row-h=48, gap=14 默认 per ADR-0016 D4). Markdown blocks rowSpan='auto' rendering-derived (ResizeObserver scrollHeight 反算行数 per ADR-0016 D3 `useAutoRowSpan` hook), 不入持久化 — 与其他 block rowSpan=integer 入持久化形成不对称. **HeavyBlockBoundary consumer (per ADR-0014 v0.5 amendment, Wave 5 Stage C.2)**: `heavyBoundaryDimensions` per-kind 必须与 colSpan/rowSpan 联动; e.g., AgentFlow `colSpan=6 rowSpan=6` → dims = `{ width: 6*(1fr)+5*14, height: 6*48+5*14 = 358 }`. 默认 fallback dims (Wave 4 ADR-0014 D5 锁定值) 仅用于 SSR 阶段 colSpan/rowSpan 未知时. **CSS application**: `style={{ gridColumn: \`${col} / span ${colSpan}\`, gridRow: row !== undefined ? \`${row} / span ${effectiveRowSpan}\` : \`span ${effectiveRowSpan}\` }}` per ADR-0016 D2. Authority: `packages/block-foundation/src/types.ts` `BlockGridPosition` interface + `COL_SNAPS` const + `BlockUIDefinition.gridDefault` + `BlockUIDefinition.rowSpanSemantic` (per ADR-0016 D10; Wave 5 Stage C.2 实施). Consumers: `apps/site` Astro renderer (per ADR-0016 D8) + `@skb/editor-shell` grid container + `@skb/mdx-bridge` serialize/parse (per ADR-0016 D7).
packages/heavy-block-boundary/CONTRACT.md:43:- **Reduced-motion (A4; AC#14)**: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` query disables spinner animation. CSS file `src/heavy-block-skeleton.css` is the canonical source.

docs/decisions/ADR-0012-search-index-stack.md:86:- **`/search` route SSR-before-index render-safety** — D3 范畴。astro-pagefind post-build hook 在 `astro build` HTML emit 之后才生成 `dist/pagefind/`；理论上 `/search` 静态 HTML 在打包过程中无法引用尚未存在的索引。D3 PLAN 必须 lock：(a) `/search` 客户端脚本 lazy-load PageFind runtime + index，HTML 静态壳允许 fallback 文案 "搜索就绪中" 在 fetch 完成前显示；(b) 静态预渲染期间不调用 PageFind API。本 ADR 不预言 D3 实现细节（plan-challenger D1b #C4(a) noted as out-of-scope）。
docs/decisions/ADR-0012-search-index-stack.md:117:PageFind 1.5+ segments at INDEX time via `Intl.Segmenter`（per `## Decision` §1 + `## Acceptance criteria` 4 D1a evidence — `笔记本电脑` 拆为 `['笔记本', '电脑']`），但其 RUNTIME query parser 对 indexed tokens 仍应用 partial-substring matching：query `"记本"` 命中 `"笔记本电脑"` note，因为 `"记本"` 是 `"笔记本"` segment 的子串。这是 PageFind 原生 runtime 行为（与 PageFind 跨语言 fuzzy-match 哲学一致），**不是项目缺陷**。Wave 3 Stage D D3 vitest `search-cjk.test.ts` 在 CI 2026-05-01 首次发现该 inverse-discriminator runtime 不成立，详细评估见 memory `feedback_pagefind_query_substring.md`，并在 [ADR-0013](ADR-0013-wave-3-close.md) D3 列为 Wave 4 mandatory 携带项。
docs/decisions/ADR-0012-search-index-stack.md:119:**`## Acceptance criteria` 4 (CJK regression test) 修订**：原 inverse 断言（`"记本"` MUST NOT match `"笔记本电脑"`）作 **index-tokenizer 层** discriminator 仍然有效（保持 word-level 与 char-level tokenizer 选型分辨；ADR-0013 D3 仍以此区分 PageFind ≥1.5 vs flexsearch `Charset.CJK`）。但 **runtime 层** 的 inverse 断言不可由 PageFind 单方面强制 —— `pagefind.search("记本")` 会返回包含 `"笔记本电脑"` 片段的结果集。项目 Wave 4 mitigation **锁路径 (b) 自定义 query parser**（apps/site 客户端 word-level 过滤层；PageFind 索引产物不变，只在结果展示前过滤）。**path (a) waive** 的方案 user-gatekeeper 已驳回（CJK 笔记 UX 期望 word-level，substring 兜底视为缺陷）。

docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:81:### D3 — Tiebreak 距离公式 (overlapping edge rects in gap region)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:85:距离公式 (per Q3 absorbtion — signed scalar via 距离公式表 + abs(distance) comparison + velocity 公式化):
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:106:  blockBounds: { left: number; top: number };  // for 0-velocity spatial fallback
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:109:type DragVelocity = { vx: number; vy: number };  // px/frame at 16ms; ε = 0.5
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:111:function tiebreak(matches: EdgeMatch[], velocity: DragVelocity): EdgeMatch | null {
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:120:  // Step 2: velocity 主轴方向优先 (|velocity| > ε = 0.5px/frame)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:121:  const speed = Math.hypot(velocity.vx, velocity.vy);
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:123:    const dominantAxis = Math.abs(velocity.vx) > Math.abs(velocity.vy) ? 'x' : 'y';
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:124:    const dominantSign = dominantAxis === 'x' ? Math.sign(velocity.vx) : Math.sign(velocity.vy);
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:140:  // Step 3: cursor velocity ≈ 0 (≤ 0.5px/frame 抖动抑制) → 空间优先 (左上 wins) + blockId 字典序 final stable fallback
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:160:抖动抑制 ε = 0.5px/frame (per Q3 absorbtion): velocity 模长 ≤ 0.5 时认为 cursor "几乎静止", 跳过 velocity-based tiebreak 直接走 spatial fallback (空间优先 — x-axis 模式比 block.left, y-axis 模式比 block.top, 较小 wins; 同 spatial 时 blockId 字典序 stable 终止). 避免 cursor 微抖动引发 mode 抖动切换 + spatial fallback 保证 "左上优先" 用户直觉一致.
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:163:- cursor 在 gap 中心 (距 A.right = 7, 距 B.left = 7; 距离同): tiebreak 退化, 默认选 split-right on A (左侧 host 优先, 实施约定; 实际 cursor 在 0-velocity 时几乎不可能精确命中正中)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:168:**0-velocity 中心命中**: 实际 user 拖动 cursor 必有 velocity vector. 同 distance 时优先 cursor 移动方向上的 edge (e.g., 向右拖时 split-left on B; 向左拖时 split-right on A). 若 velocity ≈ 0 (frozen cursor) → spatial fallback (per `tiebreak()` Step 3): x-axis 模式 (split-left/right) 比 block.left 较小 wins; y-axis 模式 (split-top/bottom) 比 block.top 较小 wins; 同 spatial 时 blockId 字典序 final stable. 即 "左上 host 优先" 在 spatial 上确定, 不依赖 blockId 字典序与位置的关系.
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:176:| outline 类型 | 视觉 | 触发 | source position |

docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:205:**视觉反射统一约束层 (per Q1 absorbtion partial — D4/D9/D10/D11 间统一约束)**:
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:207:drag/drop UX 视觉元素 (outline overlay D4 + col-ruler D9 + drag-ghost D10 + drop-pulse D11) 共享:
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:210:- 展示状态机: `drag-active` (D6 drag-start 起) / `resize-active` (D9 resize 起) / `drop-pulse-active` (D11 720ms) — 同 stage 仅 1 active state
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:219:drag-start 时给每个 snapshot block (per D6 lift 后的快照, 不含源块) 算 4 个 edge rects:
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:232:drag-over 时遍历所有 edge rects 做"点-在-矩形"测试; 多个命中按 D3 tiebreak. per-event O(n) where n = block 数; n=30 时 < 0.05ms/frame (60fps preserved).
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:244:### D6 — 源块 lift 模式 (drag-start 时源块从 grid lift; 视觉消失)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:261:- 不执行任何 layoutMutation (NO `'drag'` source, NO `'drag-cancel'` source)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:264:- 源块 lift 撤销 (per D6); 源块恢复原 grid position
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:276:- drag-end 落定: layoutMutation `source = 'drag'` 仅 mutate col/colSpan/row; rowSpan='auto' 由 ResizeObserver 在 落定 后 (per ADR-0016 D3 两阶段稳态) 重新测量
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:280:视觉跳变 mitigation: outline 显示的是 "drag-start 瞬间高度" (frozen); drop 后实际高度可能 Δ ≤ 几行 (用户编辑期间内容长度变化通常很小). 为零跳变需要 (a) drag 中持续 ResizeObserver-track + outline 跟随 — 抖动风险高; (b) drag-end 后强制 ResizeObserver 立即重测 (per D3 两阶段稳态 first RO 触发) + 180ms transition. 选 (b) 路径; 抖动 mitigated by 两阶段稳态规则.
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:282:### D7 — `useAutoRowSpan` hook (consume ADR-0016 D3)
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:284:drag/drop UX 操作 markdown 块 (kind='prose' OR rowSpanSemantic='auto') 时 rowSpan 由 `useAutoRowSpan` hook (per ADR-0016 D3 + 两阶段稳态) 反算. drag/drop 仅 mutate `col / colSpan / row` (持久化字段); rowSpan='auto' 不入持久化, 由 ResizeObserver 在 drop 后重新测量 rendering-derived.
docs/decisions/ADR-0017-drag-drop-ux.md:286:**冲突仲裁** (per ADR-0016 D12 layoutEpoch reducer): drag-end 时 layoutMutation `source = 'drag'` 优先于 `source = 'auto-measure'` (per ADR-0016 D12 表格); 即 drag 落定瞬间 rowSpan 由 D5 transition 状态机决定 (T2 freeze + T4 重新测量).

docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:149:**No CONTRACT.md drift detected for Wave 1 or Wave 2 packages.** Every claimed export resolves to actual source. The 4-way BlockKind expansion is structurally consistent across all 8 block packages, all 3 editor sub-modules, the foundation registry, RFC.md §1, and ADR-0009.
docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:272:- `packages/<pkg>/tsconfig.json#references` contains `../design-tokens` in both packages (i.e., the asymmetry is present at package.json + tsconfig levels, but absent at TS source-import level).
docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:274:**Root cause analysis**: both packages are codex-clones of `block-callout` (Tracks C5a / C5b, commit `2601e3a`). The template `block-callout` legitimately imports `@skb/design-tokens` for the type-only `ColorTokenName` in `variant-tokens.ts` (callout uses 4 variant→token mappings: note/tip/warning/danger). `block-code` and `block-image` have **no variant-tokens equivalent**: they consume design-tokens **only as CSS variables** (`var(--color-fg)` / `var(--color-border)` in `code.css` / `image.css`). CSS variable consumption is a runtime dependency, not a TS-source dependency, so it does not produce `from '@skb/design-tokens'` import statements.
docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:287:**F3 also surfaces a meta-class candidate**: this is a **clone-time replication asymmetry**. The codex-block-generator produced a structural sister of block-callout, but the TS-import that was load-bearing in callout (anchoring the design-tokens dep claim) was not load-bearing in the clones (which had no `variant-tokens` analog). The asymmetry is not in the generator's mistake but in the source vs. clone runtime-dependency profiles. This is functionally similar to ADR-0006 item #5 (algorithm + runtime constant replication audit) but at the **inter-package package.json grain** rather than the inline-script grain. Wave 3 will see more clones (block-callout-style generator outputs); the rule extension would say: "when codex-block-generator clones a template, it must verify each template-side `@skb/*` dep has a clone-side TS import — if absent, either remove the dep or add a synthetic import".
docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:299:   - (b) Amend ADR-0008 D1 (or land a fresh ADR-N) to specify how CSS-variable consumption interacts with the dead-dep rule. Without amendment, the rule is binary "TS-import or violation" and any future `block-*` clone built on a CSS-only consumption pattern will repeat F3. Recommended language: "TS source `from '@skb/<pkg>'` OR matching `<pkg>/.../style.css` import in the package OR CONTRACT-documented CSS-variable consumption with named tokens listed". Ratify before Wave 3 first PR opens (analogous to ADR-0008 D1 freeze before Wave 2 first PR).
docs/audits/structure-2026-05.md:334:- **WE-002 lockfile-as-derivative**: codified into ADR-0006 sub-form C and applied at commit-time; no source-tree side-effect.
